1	# Notes
2	
3	This directory contains various notes and documentation related to the project.
4	Each file is organized by topic for easy reference.
5	
6	By default there are chores-*.md and todo.md. Chores are general notes
7	about tasks and todo.md contains short term tasks and their status.
8	
9	In the future we I expect we may want to create a "notes"
10	database to better manage the information, TBD.
11	
12	Examples chore file:
13	```
14	# Chores-01.md
15	
16	General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
17	more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
18	how and why of a particular chore. The section header is short and sweet
19	and the title is appended with the version number of the app when the chore
20	is completed.
21	
22	## Create an app that does something interesting (0.1.0)
23	
24	The app counts from 1 to 100, not to interesting.
25	```
26	
27	## Commit Workflow
28	
29	This project uses a per-step commit / push / finalize flow against
30	two paired repos (app + bot session). Full rules live in
31	[CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md#commit-push-finalize-flow) — that file is
32	the source of truth (the bot loads it automatically; humans can
33	read it from there too).
34	
35	## jj tips
36	
37	For users new to jj see [jj-tips.md](jj-tips.md).
38	
39	```
40	## Chores format
41	
42	Filename: "chores-XX.md"
43	example: chores-01.md
44	
45	Format of section labels: "## <short description> (X.Y.Z)"
46	example: "## Topic format description (0.1.0)"
47	
48	Example chore file:
49	```
50	# Chores-01.md
51	 
52	General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
53	more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
54	how and why of a particular chore.
55	
56	## Do something (1.3.1)
57	
58	Describe something
59	```
60	
61	## Versioning during development
62	
63	This is using jujustiu, jj + git and we'll see how it goes. Below is my
64	git workflow, jj will be different but we'll have to discover that as
65	we go.
66	
67	Every plan must start with a version bump. Choose the approach based on scope:
68	
69	- **Single-step** (recommended for mechanical/focused changes): bump directly to
70	  `X.Y.Z`, implement in one commit. Simpler history.
71	- **Multi-step** (for exploratory/large changes): bump to `X.Y.Z-devN`, implement
72	  across multiple commits, final commit removes `-devN`.
73	
74	The plan should recommend one approach and get user approval before starting.
75	
76	For multi-step:
77	1. Bump version to `X.Y.Z-devN` with a plan and commit as a chore marker
78	2. Implement in one or more `-devN` commits (bump N as needed)
79	3. Final commit removes `-devN`, updates todo/chores — this is the "done" marker
80	
81	The final release commit (without `-devN`) signals completion rather than amending
82	prior commits. This keeps the git history readable and makes it easy to see which
83	commits were exploratory vs final.
84	
85	## Todo format
86	
87	Todo.md contains two main sections "Todo" and "Done" each item is a
88	short explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more
89	references.
90	
91	Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
92	In markdown, `[2,3]` is a single ref key (won't resolve) and `[2][3]`
93	is parsed as display text `2` with ref key `3` (so `[2]` won't resolve).
94	
95	Examples:
96	
97	# Todo
98	
99	- Add new feature X [details](chores-01.md#feature-x)
100	- Fix bug Y [1]
101	
102	# Done
103	
104	- Fixed issue Z [2],[3]
105	
106	[1]: chores-01bugs.md#bug-y
107	
```

```
1	# iiac-perf
2	
3	Measure performance of various Inter-Intra Application Communication
4	(IIAC) techniques.
5	
6	## Design (0.2.0)
7	
8	Design a Rust app that can measure the cost of various (IIAC) techniques.
9	By IIAC I mean all communication techniques between and within
10	applications such as regular function calls vs async function calls vs
11	channels vs serde/deser of json or zero-copy messaging using io_uring and
12	potentially any other technique. It would include between threads or
13	processes or apps on the same computer or between apps on the same LAN or
14	between apps over a WAN.
15	
16	Ideally I'd like to be able to see a histogram of the range of times of the
17	send and receive costs, not just the mean/average of 10^3 or 10^6 invocations.
18	So the cost of the work should or could be something near-zero such as just
19	echoing the input, but maybe there is value in doing some constant but
20	variable amount of work to verify it's in-consequential. One thing I think we
21	need to be aware of is that if we make the work too simple the compiler could
22	just keep things in registers which would not be representative of "real" work.
23	
24	We should start simple, like comparing normal and async function calls and
25	later expand to other techniques.
26	
27	## Usage
28	
29	```
30	iiac-perf [BENCH...] [-d SECONDS] [-o OUTER] [-i INNER]
31	```
32	
33	`BENCH` is one or more registered bench names, or `all` for every
34	registered bench. **With no arguments, `iiac-perf` prints the
35	available list and exits — that's the source of truth for which
36	benches the current build registers.**
37	
38	Flags (also visible via `-h` / `--help`):
39	- `-d`, `--duration SECONDS` — target wall-clock seconds per bench
40	  (default `5.0`); the outer loop runs until this time is reached
41	  (inner auto-sizes). See chores `0.3.1-dev1` for the empirical
42	  study behind the default — longer (`-d 30`+) gives
43	  publication-grade stability. Mutually exclusive with `-D`.
44	- `-D`, `--total-duration SECONDS` — target total wall-clock seconds
45	  across all requested benches; budget is split equally per bench
46	  (e.g. `-D 30` with 6 benches → 5 s each). Mutually exclusive with
47	  `-d`.
48	- `-o`, `--outer N` — override outer loop count (forces count-based
49	  mode instead of time-based; inner still adapts).
50	- `-i`, `--inner N` — override inner loop count per histogram sample.
51	  `inner=1` measures single-call latency (each sample = one step).
52	  Higher inner measures back-to-back / burst rate (each sample = N
53	  steps averaged, hides per-call jitter and parking costs).
54	- `--pin CORES` — pin bench threads to logical CPUs. `CORES` is a
55	  comma-separated list with optional ranges: `0,1`, `0-5`, `0,3-5,7`.
56	  Treated as a **core pool** indexed positionally with wrap-around, so
57	  thread `i` gets `pool[i % pool.len()]`. Examples:
58	  - `--pin 0,1` pins a 2-thread bench to logical CPUs 0 and 1.
59	  - `--pin 0,0` co-locates two threads on the same CPU
60	    (oversubscription — measures contention).
61	  - `--pin 0-11` defines a 12-CPU pool for larger fanout benches;
62	    threads wrap over it.
63	
64	  On AMD Zen 2 (e.g. Ryzen 9 3900X, 12 physical × 2 SMT = 24 logical
65	  CPUs), logical IDs `N` and `N+12` are SMT siblings of the same
66	  physical core. `--pin 0,12` pairs siblings (max resource contention);
67	  `--pin 0,1` uses independent physical cores in the same CCX (best
68	  latency for channel benches — shared L3, no SMT contention). Check
69	  your topology with
70	  `cat /sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu0/topology/thread_siblings_list`.
71	
72	  Typical measured effect on `mpsc-2t` at `-d 10` (3900X, idle desktop):
73	  unpinned mean ≈ 7,044 ns / stdev ≈ 6,545 ns / p99.99 ≈ 74 µs;
74	  `--pin 0,1` → mean ≈ 5,636 ns / stdev ≈ 1,321 ns / p99.99 ≈ 17 µs.
75	  Tail tightens ~4×, stdev ~5×, mean drops ~20 %.
76	
77	Each bench prints a band-based histogram in nanoseconds. Bands are
78	defined by percentile boundaries (min→p1, p1→p10, …, p99→max) and
79	show first, last, range (`last - first + 1`), count, mean, and
80	adjusted mean. The adjusted column subtracts apparatus overhead
81	(`framing_per_sample / inner + loop_per_iter`), calibrated once at
82	startup via a two-point fit on an empty bench.
83	
84	Examples:
85	
86	```
87	iiac-perf                        # list available benches
88	iiac-perf all                    # every bench, default ~5s each
89	iiac-perf min-now -d 30          # one bench, 30s budget
90	iiac-perf all -D 30              # ~30s total split equally
91	iiac-perf mpsc-2t -i 1           # explicit single-call latency
92	iiac-perf mpsc-2t -i 100         # back-to-back rate
93	iiac-perf mpsc-2t --pin 0,1      # pinned, different physical cores
94	iiac-perf mpsc-2t --pin 0,12     # pinned, SMT siblings (contention)
95	```
96	
97	## Testing
98	
99	```
100	cargo test                                    # normal run
101	cargo test -- --nocapture                     # show eprintln diagnostics
102	taskset -c 0 cargo test -- --nocapture        # restrict to 1 CPU
103	```
104	
105	The `pin_current_can_switch_cores` test verifies that CPU pinning
106	works after a prior pin (the bug fixed in 0.3.6). It uses
107	`sched_getaffinity` to detect available CPUs, so under `taskset -c 0`
108	it skips gracefully rather than failing. Use `--nocapture` to see
109	which path was taken.
110	
111	## Workflow
112	
113	Commits, pushes, and finalizes follow a per-step checkpoint flow
114	designed for this dual-repo (app + `.claude` bot session) setup.
115	See [CLAUDE.md](CLAUDE.md#commit-push-finalize-flow) for the full
116	spec — single source of truth so the bot can't drift from the
117	human docs.
118	
119	## Convention
120	
121	This is the main repo of a dual-repo convention for using
122	a bot to help in the development of a coding project. The goal
123	is that this main repo contains the "what", while the partner
124	bot repo contains "why" and "how". The key to the convention
125	is each change is cross-referenced to the other. Thus there
126	is a coherent story of the development of the project across time.
127	
128	The beginnings of that tool is [vc-x1](https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1)
129	which currently does achieve this goal, but is being used as a
130	first test bed.
131	
132	## Cloning
133	
134	Use [vc-x1](https://github.com/winksaville/vc-x1) to clone
135	the dual-repo project. It handles `git clone --recursive`,
136	`jj` init for both repos, and the Claude Code symlink:
137	
138	```
139	vc-x1 clone winksaville/iiac-perf
140	```
141	
142	## jj Tips for Git Users
143	
144	See [notes/jj-tips](notes/jj-tips.md)
145	
146	## License
147	
148	Licensed under either of
149	
150	- Apache License, Version 2.0 ([LICENSE-APACHE](LICENSE-APACHE) or http://apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0)
151	- MIT license ([LICENSE-MIT](LICENSE-MIT) or http://opensource.org/licenses/MIT)
152	
153	### Contribution
154	
155	Unless you explicitly state otherwise, any contribution intentionally submitted
156	for inclusion in the work by you, as defined in the Apache-2.0 license, shall
157	be dual licensed as above, without any additional terms or conditions.
158	
159	[1]: https://github.com/karpathy/autoresearch
160	
```

1	use std::hint::black_box;
2	
3	use crate::harness::Bench;
4	
5	const CAL_WARMUP: u64 = 1000;
6	const CAL_SAMPLES: u64 = 10_000;
7	const N_LOW: u64 = 100;
8	const N_HIGH: u64 = 1000;
9	
10	#[derive(Debug)]
11	pub struct Overhead {
12	    pub framing_per_sample_ns: f64,
13	    pub loop_per_iter_ns: f64,
14	}
15	
16	impl Overhead {
17	    pub fn per_call_ns(&self, inner: u64) -> f64 {
18	        self.framing_per_sample_ns / inner as f64 + self.loop_per_iter_ns
19	    }
20	}
21	
22	struct EmptyBench;
23	
24	impl Bench for EmptyBench {
25	    fn name(&self) -> &str {
26	        "empty"
27	    }
28	
29	    fn step(&mut self) -> u64 {
30	        black_box(1)
31	    }
32	}
33	
34	pub fn calibrate() -> Overhead {
35	    let mut bench = EmptyBench;
36	    for _ in 0..CAL_WARMUP {
37	        black_box(bench.step());
38	    }
39	
40	    let min_low = measure(&mut bench, CAL_SAMPLES, N_LOW);
41	    let min_high = measure(&mut bench, CAL_SAMPLES, N_HIGH);
42	
43	    // Two-point fit: per_sample = framing + inner * loop_per_iter.
44	    // Slope between (N_LOW, min_low) and (N_HIGH, min_high) gives loop_per_iter,
45	    // intercept gives framing. Cancels TSC pipelining of the framing pair.
46	    let loop_per_iter_ns = if min_high > min_low {
47	        (min_high - min_low) as f64 / (N_HIGH - N_LOW) as f64
48	    } else {
49	        0.0
50	    };
51	    let framing_per_sample_ns = (min_low as f64 - N_LOW as f64 * loop_per_iter_ns).max(0.0);
52	
53	    Overhead {
54	        framing_per_sample_ns,
55	        loop_per_iter_ns,
56	    }
57	}
58	
59	fn measure(bench: &mut EmptyBench, samples: u64, inner: u64) -> u64 {
60	    let mut min_ns = u64::MAX;
61	    for _ in 0..samples {
62	        let start = minstant::Instant::now();
63	        for _ in 0..inner {
64	            black_box(bench.step());
65	        }
66	        let e = start.elapsed().as_nanos() as u64;
67	        if e < min_ns {
68	            min_ns = e;
69	        }
70	    }
71	    min_ns
72	}
73	
```

> AGENT

The exact 2× is arithmetic, not physical. Two-point fit has:

```
framing = min_low − N_LOW · (min_high − min_low) / (N_HIGH − N_LOW)
d(framing)/d(min_low) = N_HIGH / (N_HIGH − N_LOW) = 1000/900 ≈ 1.11
```

Reverse-engineering your numbers: run 1 had `min_low=60, min_high=500` → framing 11.11. Run 2 had `min_low=70, min_high=500` → framing 22.22. Only `min_low` moved, by ~10 ns, which the fit amplifies 1.11× into +11 ns on framing. The "2×" is just 11 + 11. So the real problem isn't a 2× cost — it's that `min_low` picked up ~10 ns of slop while `min_high` stayed clean.

Why `min_low` is noisy but `min_high` isn't: calibration runs on the **unpinned main thread** and `measure(N_LOW=100)` is called first, right after a mere 1000-iter warmup. That's ~500 µs of work — too short to guarantee full boost-clock ramp. By the time `measure(N_HIGH=1000)` runs, the CPU is at peak, so `min_high` reliably hits its ~500 ns floor. `min_low`'s floor varies between runs depending on how far the boost ramp had progressed.

The fit is also structurally fragile here: with only 10k samples and a 10× inner-size ratio, the slope/intercept are very sensitive to small endpoint noise, and the `black_box(1)` body is so cheap that `min_low` is dominated by framing rather than loop cost — exactly where we're trying to measure.

Ranked fixes (pick one or combine):
1. **Pin the main thread** to the first `--pin` core for calibration (same thermal/boost state as the bench). Single biggest coherence win — calibration should run under the conditions the bench runs under.
2. **Longer warmup + more samples**, e.g. `CAL_WARMUP=100_000`, `CAL_SAMPLES=100_000`. Cheap, ensures boost ramp and gives min a better shot at its floor.
3. **Wider N spread** (`N_LOW=10, N_HIGH=10_000`) drops the noise-amplification coefficient from 1.11× to ~1.01×, so any remaining noise barely perturbs framing.
4. **Sanity-check loop** — run calibration N=3–5 times, require framing/loop to be within e.g. 20% tolerance; retry or warn otherwise. Belt-and-suspenders.

My recommendation: do (1) + (2) first — they address the root cause (calibration running under different conditions than the bench, before boost) rather than papering over it. (3) is a nice-to-have robustness improvement. Which direction do you want to take?

1	use std::collections::BTreeSet;
2	
3	/// Parse a `--pin` value (comma-separated list with optional ranges) into
4	/// an ordered vector of logical CPU IDs. Accepts forms like `"0,1"`,
5	/// `"0-11"`, `"0,3-5,7"`. Duplicates are preserved in the given order so
6	/// oversubscription (`"0,0,0"`) works.
7	pub fn parse_cores(spec: &str) -> Result<Vec<usize>, String> {
8	    let mut out = Vec::new();
9	    for part in spec.split(',').map(str::trim).filter(|s| !s.is_empty()) {
10	        match part.split_once('-') {
11	            None => out.push(
12	                part.parse::<usize>()
13	                    .map_err(|e| format!("invalid core id {part:?}: {e}"))?,
14	            ),
15	            Some((lo, hi)) => {
16	                let lo = lo
17	                    .trim()
18	                    .parse::<usize>()
19	                    .map_err(|e| format!("invalid range start {lo:?}: {e}"))?;
20	                let hi = hi
21	                    .trim()
22	                    .parse::<usize>()
23	                    .map_err(|e| format!("invalid range end {hi:?}: {e}"))?;
24	                if hi < lo {
25	                    return Err(format!("range {lo}-{hi} is empty"));
26	                }
27	                out.extend(lo..=hi);
28	            }
29	        }
30	    }
31	    Ok(out)
32	}
33	
34	/// Print the current thread's CPU id to stderr with a label.
35	/// Useful for debugging pinning — not called in normal paths.
36	#[allow(dead_code)]
37	pub fn print_core_id(prompt: &str) {
38	    let cid = unsafe { libc::sched_getcpu() };
39	    eprintln!("{prompt}: cid={cid}");
40	}
41	
42	/// Pin the current thread to `logical_cpu`. No-op if `None`.
43	///
44	/// Constructs a `CoreId` directly rather than querying
45	/// `get_core_ids()`, which only returns cores in the caller's
46	/// current affinity mask — after the first pin that mask is
47	/// narrowed and subsequent lookups for other cores would fail.
48	pub fn pin_current(logical_cpu: Option<usize>) {
49	    let Some(target) = logical_cpu else { return };
50	    core_affinity::set_for_current(core_affinity::CoreId { id: target });
51	}
52	
53	/// Report the pinning plan (what the user asked for) as a human-readable
54	/// summary for the startup banner.
55	pub fn plan_summary(cores: &[usize]) -> String {
56	    if cores.is_empty() {
57	        return "none (unpinned)".to_string();
58	    }
59	    let unique: BTreeSet<usize> = cores.iter().copied().collect();
60	    format!(
61	        "{cores:?} ({} slot{}, {} unique CPU{})",
62	        cores.len(),
63	        if cores.len() == 1 { "" } else { "s" },
64	        unique.len(),
65	        if unique.len() == 1 { "" } else { "s" },
66	    )
67	}
68	
69	#[cfg(test)]
70	mod tests {
71	    use super::*;
72	
73	    #[test]
74	    fn parse_plain_list() {
75	        assert_eq!(parse_cores("0,1,2").unwrap(), vec![0, 1, 2]);
76	    }
77	
78	    #[test]
79	    fn parse_range() {
80	        assert_eq!(parse_cores("0-3").unwrap(), vec![0, 1, 2, 3]);
81	    }
82	
83	    #[test]
84	    fn parse_mixed() {
85	        assert_eq!(parse_cores("0,3-5,7").unwrap(), vec![0, 3, 4, 5, 7]);
86	    }
87	
88	    #[test]
89	    fn parse_duplicates_preserved() {
90	        assert_eq!(parse_cores("0,0,0").unwrap(), vec![0, 0, 0]);
91	    }
92	
93	    #[test]
94	    fn parse_empty_string_ok() {
95	        assert_eq!(parse_cores("").unwrap(), Vec::<usize>::new());
96	    }
97	
98	    #[test]
99	    fn parse_reverse_range_errs() {
100	        assert!(parse_cores("5-3").is_err());
101	    }
102	
103	    #[test]
104	    fn parse_garbage_errs() {
105	        assert!(parse_cores("abc").is_err());
106	        assert!(parse_cores("1-x").is_err());
107	    }
108	
109	    #[test]
110	    fn pin_current_can_switch_cores() {
111	        let mut set = unsafe { std::mem::zeroed::<libc::cpu_set_t>() };
112	        let ret = unsafe { libc::sched_getaffinity(0, size_of::<libc::cpu_set_t>(), &mut set) };
113	        if ret != 0 {
114	            eprintln!("can't query affinity");
115	            return; // can't query affinity
116	        }
117	        let available = unsafe { libc::CPU_COUNT(&set) } as usize;
118	        if available < 2 {
119	            eprintln!("single-core or restricted by taskset");
120	            return; // single-core or restricted by taskset
121	        }
122	        let a = 0;
123	        let b = 1;
124	
125	        pin_current(Some(a));
126	        super::print_core_id("after pin to a");
127	        let cpu_a = unsafe { libc::sched_getcpu() } as usize;
128	        assert_eq!(cpu_a, a);
129	
130	        pin_current(Some(b));
131	        super::print_core_id("after pin to b");
132	        let cpu_b = unsafe { libc::sched_getcpu() } as usize;
133	        assert_eq!(cpu_b, b);
134	    }
135	}
136

1	mod benches;
2	mod harness;
3	mod overhead;
4	mod pin;
5	
6	use clap::Parser;
7	
8	#[derive(Parser)]
9	#[command(version, about = "IIAC performance measurement", max_term_width = 80)]
10	struct Cli {
11	    /// Benches to run. Pass 'all' for every registered bench, or
12	    /// one or more names. Run with no args to see the available list.
13	    benches: Vec<String>,
14	
15	    /// Target wall-clock seconds per bench (default 5.0). Auto-sizes
16	    /// outer and inner loop counts. Mutually exclusive with -D.
17	    #[arg(short = 'd', long, conflicts_with = "total_duration")]
18	    duration: Option<f64>,
19	
20	    /// Target total wall-clock seconds across all requested benches;
21	    /// budget is split equally per bench. Mutually exclusive with -d.
22	    #[arg(short = 'D', long)]
23	    total_duration: Option<f64>,
24	
25	    /// Override outer loop count (skips auto-sizing; inner still adapts).
26	    #[arg(short, long)]
27	    outer: Option<u64>,
28	
29	    /// Override inner loop count (skips auto-sizing).
30	    /// inner=1 measures single-call latency (each sample = one step); higher
31	    /// inner measures back-to-back/burst rate (each sample = N steps averaged).
32	    #[arg(short, long)]
33	    inner: Option<u64>,
34	
35	    /// Pin bench threads to logical CPUs (comma-separated, ranges OK).
36	    /// The list is a core *pool*: thread `i` of a bench is pinned to
37	    /// `pool[i % pool.len()]`, so shorter pools oversubscribe by wrap.
38	    /// Examples: `--pin 0,1` (2 threads → 2 CPUs), `--pin 0-5` (6-thread
39	    /// pool), `--pin 0,0` (two threads on the same CPU). On 3900X,
40	    /// logical CPUs N and N+12 are SMT siblings of the same physical
41	    /// core — `--pin 0,12` pairs siblings (max contention), `--pin 0,1`
42	    /// gives independent cores. Omit to leave threads unpinned.
43	    #[arg(long, value_name = "CORES")]
44	    pin: Option<String>,
45	}
46	
47	const DEFAULT_DURATION: f64 = 5.0;
48	
49	fn main() {
50	    let cli = Cli::parse();
51	
52	    if cli.benches.is_empty() {
53	        println!("no benches specified. use -h or --help for more info.");
54	        println!("available: all, {}", benches::names().join(", "));
55	        return;
56	    }
57	
58	    println!(
59	        "iiac-perf {} — IIAC performance measurement\n",
60	        env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION")
61	    );
62	
63	    let pin_cores: Vec<usize> = match cli.pin.as_deref() {
64	        None => Vec::new(),
65	        Some(spec) => match pin::parse_cores(spec) {
66	            Ok(v) => v,
67	            Err(e) => {
68	                eprintln!("error: --pin: {e}");
69	                std::process::exit(2);
70	            }
71	        },
72	    };
73	
74	    // Pin the main (measurement) thread before any calibration or bench
75	    // work so TSC reads stay on the same CPU across start/elapsed.
76	    if let Some(&cpu) = pin_cores.first() {
77	        pin::pin_current(Some(cpu));
78	    }
79	
80	    let overhead = overhead::calibrate();
81	    println!("Calibration:");
82	    println!(
83	        "  framing/sample    {:>7} ns  (timer pair, two-point fit)",
84	        harness::fmt_commas_f64(overhead.framing_per_sample_ns, 2)
85	    );
86	    println!(
87	        "  loop/iter         {:>7} ns  (per inner-loop iteration)",
88	        harness::fmt_commas_f64(overhead.loop_per_iter_ns, 2)
89	    );
90	    println!("  pinning           {}", pin::plan_summary(&pin_cores));
91	    println!();
92	
93	    let runners = match benches::resolve(&cli.benches) {
94	        Ok(r) => r,
95	        Err(e) => {
96	            eprintln!("error: {e}");
97	            std::process::exit(2);
98	        }
99	    };
100	
101	    let target_seconds = match (cli.duration, cli.total_duration) {
102	        (Some(d), _) => d,
103	        (None, Some(t)) => t / runners.len() as f64,
104	        (None, None) => DEFAULT_DURATION,
105	    };
106	
107	    let cfg = harness::RunCfg {
108	        overhead: &overhead,
109	        target_seconds,
110	        outer_override: cli.outer,
111	        inner_override: cli.inner,
112	        pin_cores: &pin_cores,
113	    };
114	
115	    for run in runners {
116	        run(&cfg);
117	    }
118	}
119	
```

1	# Chores-01
2	
3	Discussions and notes on various chores in github compatible markdown.
4	There is also a [todo.md](todo.md) file and it tracks tasks and in
5	general there should be a chore section for each task with the why
6	and how this task will be completed.
7	
8	See [Chores format](README.md#chores-format)
9	
10	## A dummy chore (TBD)
11	
12	A dummy chore description.
13	
14	## Measure timer overhead (0.1.0)
15	
16	Added initial Rust app that measures the overhead of `minstant::Instant::now()`
17	vs `std::time::Instant::now()`. Uses `minstant` as the reference timer for both
18	measurements and records 1M iterations into `hdrhistogram` histograms. Reports
19	min, p50, p90, p99, p99.9, p99.99, max, mean, and stdev in nanoseconds.
20	
21	This bootstraps the benchmarking project by measuring the measuring stick first,
22	validating the measurement approach before applying it to actual IIAC techniques.
23	
24	## Refactor to Bench trait + add channel bench (0.2.0)
25	
26	Restructure `iiac-perf` so new IIAC benchmarks can be added as self-contained
27	modules instead of hand-wiring each into `main`. Also add overhead calibration
28	and the first real IIAC bench: `std::sync::mpsc` round-trip on one thread.
29	
30	### Why
31	
32	The README's trajectory (async, serde, io_uring, LAN, WAN, …) implies many
33	benchmarks. A `match` in `main` would grow unwieldy. Calibrating timer and
34	loop overhead lets each bench report a per-call cost closer to the technique
35	itself rather than the measurement apparatus.
36	
37	### How
38	
39	Multi-step, target version `0.2.0`. Approach approved by user.
40	
41	Planned module layout:
42	
43	```
44	src/
45	  main.rs              # CLI: `iiac-perf [timer|channel|all] -i N`
46	  harness.rs           # Bench trait + runner + histogram printer
47	  overhead.rs          # timer + loop overhead calibration
48	  benches/
49	    mod.rs
50	    timer.rs           # minstant vs std::time::Instant
51	    channel.rs         # std::sync::mpsc round trip, single thread
52	```
53	
54	Trait sketch:
55	
56	```rust
57	pub trait Bench {
58	    fn name(&self) -> &str;
59	    fn step(&mut self);  // one unit of work; harness times N per sample
60	}
61	```
62	
63	Overhead subtraction:
64	
65	- `timer_overhead_ns` — min of back-to-back `Instant::now() / elapsed()` pairs
66	- `loop_overhead_ns`  — per-iter cost of empty `for _ in 0..N { black_box(()); }`
67	- `per_call = (elapsed - timer_overhead)/N - loop_overhead`
68	
69	Print **raw and adjusted side-by-side**. Subtracting a min from a full
70	distribution is asymmetric — near the floor, adjusted percentiles can go
71	near zero. Showing both keeps the raw data honest.
72	
73	### Dev steps
74	
75	1. `0.2.0-dev1` ✅ chore marker: bump version, write this plan, update todo.
76	   Also clarified CLAUDE.md: commit-push-finalize runs per step.
77	2. `0.2.0-dev2` ✅ refactor: `Bench` trait + `harness` module, `benches/timer.rs`.
78	   `run_bench` is generic (`<B: Bench>`) so monomorphization keeps the hot
79	   loop free of vtable dispatch — matters for a perf tool. No behavior
80	   change vs 0.1.0 output.
```

480	mpsc_2t (run, new, drop). Moved `cid` → `pin::print_core_id`
481	(pub, `#[allow(dead_code)]`) as a reusable debug helper that

486	
487	## Rename CLI flags: -iterations → -outer, -inner → -inner (0.3.7)
488	
489	Renamed `-i/--iterations` → `-o/--outer` and `-I/--inner` → `-i/--inner`
490	to align the CLI flags with what the histogram header already displays
491	(`outer=`, `inner=`). The `-o`/`-i` short flags now mirror the loop
492	levels symmetrically.
493	
494	### Change
495	
496	- CLI: `-i` → `-o`, `--iterations` → `--outer`; `-I` → `-i` (inner
497	  keeps `--inner`).
498	- Internal: `iterations_override` → `outer_override`,
499	  `MIN_ITERATIONS`/`MAX_ITERATIONS` → `MIN_OUTER`/`MAX_OUTER`,
500	  `pick_iterations` → `pick_outer`, local `iterations` vars → `outer`
501	  throughout harness and all bench `run()` functions.
502	- README Usage section updated.
503	- Breaking CLI change vs 0.3.6 — acceptable on 0.x.
504	
505	Single-step bump 0.3.6 → 0.3.7.
506	
507	## Time-based outer loop (0.4.0)
508	
509	The outer loop now runs until wall-clock time exceeds the target
510	duration (default 5s) instead of pre-computing an iteration count.
511	This is more honest — the old approach estimated step cost upfront
512	and computed `outer = target_ns / step_cost`, but step cost varies
513	during a run (thermal throttling, scheduler noise, cache effects),
514	so actual duration could drift from the target.
515	
516	### Change
517	
518	- New `run_timed` loop checks `elapsed >= target_ns` after each
519	  sample. Actual outer count comes from `hist.len()` — accurate
520	  by definition.
521	- `run_counted` preserves the old count-based loop for `-o/--outer`.
522	- Shared `record_sample` + `new_hist` helpers factored out.
523	- Removed `pick_outer`, `MIN_OUTER`, `MAX_OUTER` — no longer needed.
524	- README updated to describe time-based default.
525	
526	Single-step bump 0.3.7 → 0.4.0.
527	
528	## Add range column to histogram (0.5.0)
529	
530	Added a `range` column to the band-based histogram display, computed
531	as `last - first + 1` per band. Shows band spread at a glance —
532	tight range = clustered, wide range = high variance within that
533	percentile slice. Positioned between `last` and `count`.
534	
535	The `+1` is intentional for integer ns: `first=100, last=100` →
536	`range=1` (one distinct value, not zero spread).
537	
538	Summary rows (mean, stdev) leave the range column blank.
539	
540	Also added trimmed mean/stdev rows (`mean min-p99`, `stdev min-p99`)
541	that exclude only the p99-max band. The min-p1 band is included
542	because it represents real fast-path behavior (hot round-trips
543	before futex parking), not noise — it disappears with `--pin`.
544	Only p99-max contains kernel preemption / futex spikes. Trimmed
545	stdev computed by walking histogram buckets a second time for
546	exact per-bucket variance against the trimmed mean.
547	
548	Measured effect (3900X, `-D 10`):
549	- Pinned `--pin 5,10`: full stdev 682 ns → trimmed 348 ns
550	- Unpinned: full stdev 6,787 ns → trimmed 2,502 ns
551	
552	Single-step bump 0.4.0 → 0.5.0.
553

> AGENT

Now I have the full picture. Short answer first:

**On "should we unpin after calibration?":** functionally irrelevant. When `--pin` is set, the main thread parks in `pthread_join` during the bench, so its affinity doesn't compete with anything. The *actually useful* related move is the **inverse**: pin main *for calibration* even when the user didn't specify `--pin`. The empirical gap is stark: with `--pin 5,10` calibration gives framing 11.11 ns / stdev 787 ns / p99.99 tail 272 µs; without `--pin`, framing jumps to **27.78 ns** and tail blows out to **5.5 ms**. The noise doesn't come from the bench — it comes from running calibration unpinned.

So the cleaner contract is: calibration always runs pinned (stable environment), bench runs per `--pin` (user's choice), main's pinning during bench is a don't-care. Unpinning after calibration is neither necessary nor harmful; skip unless a concrete reason shows up later.

Now writing chores-02 + updating todo.md.

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/notes/chores-02.md",
  "content": "# Chores-02\n\nContinuation of `chores-01.md`, which crossed 500 lines. Same format;\nsee [Chores format](README.md#chores-format).\n\n## Calibration robustness (0.6.0)\n\nCalibration in `overhead.rs` occasionally produces a framing value\nexactly 2× the typical — e.g. 11.11 ns one run, 22.22 ns the next,\non `iiac-perf mpsc-2t --pin 5,10 -D 1` (3900X). Not a physical 2×\neffect — it's the arithmetic of the two-point fit amplifying ~10 ns\nof endpoint noise.\n\n### Analysis\n\nTwo-point fit:\n\n```\nframing  = min_low − N_LOW · (min_high − min_low) / (N_HIGH − N_LOW)\nd(framing)/d(min_low)  = N_HIGH / (N_HIGH − N_LOW) = 1000 / 900 ≈ 1.11\nd(framing)/d(min_high) = −N_LOW / (N_HIGH − N_LOW) = −100 / 900 ≈ −0.11\n```\n\nReverse-engineering two observed runs (3900X, `--pin 5,10`, `-D 1`):\n\n- Run A: `min_low = 60 ns, min_high = 500 ns` → framing 11.11 ns,\n  loop/iter 0.4889 ns.\n- Run B: `min_low = 70 ns, min_high = 500 ns` → framing 22.22 ns,\n  loop/iter 0.4778 ns.\n\nOnly `min_low` moved (+10 ns). The fit amplified that by 10/9 = 1.11\n→ +11.11 ns on framing. 11.11 + 11.11 = 22.22. The \"exact 2×\" is\nnumerology; the real defect is ~10 ns of slop on `min_low`.\n\nWhy `min_low` floats but `min_high` is stable: `measure(N_LOW=100)`\nruns first, right after a mere 1,000-iter warmup (~500 µs of work).\nThat's too short to guarantee the CPU has reached peak boost. By the\ntime `measure(N_HIGH=1000)` runs, boost is fully ramped, so\n`min_high` reliably hits its ~500 ns floor.\n\n### --pin vs unpinned main thread\n\n`main.rs` already pins the main thread to `pin_cores.first()` before\ncalibration. Measured on 3900X, `-d 3`:\n\n| configuration             | framing    | stdev      | p99.99 tail |\n|---------------------------|-----------:|-----------:|------------:|\n| `--pin 5,10`              |  11.11 ns  |    787 ns  |    272 µs   |\n| no `--pin` (main unpinned)|  27.78 ns  | 12,967 ns  |    5.5 ms   |\n\nSo when `--pin` is omitted, calibration itself inherits the unpinned\njitter (migrations, frequency wobble, CCX changes), and the framing\nnumber is clearly elevated beyond the true floor. The adjustment we\nsubtract is then derived from a different measurement regime than the\none we want.\n\n### Should main be unpinned after calibration?\n\nFunctionally irrelevant. When bench threads run, the main thread\nblocks in `pthread_join` — it's parked, not runnable, so its affinity\ndoesn't compete with anything. Keeping or dropping the pin changes\nnothing observable.\n\nThe cleaner contract is:\n\n- Calibration always runs pinned (stable environment, reliable\n  framing/loop numbers).\n- Bench runs per `--pin` (user's requested environment).\n- Main's pinning during the bench phase is a don't-care.\n\nSo: leave main's pin state alone after calibration; don't add a\nceremonial unpin step unless a concrete reason emerges later.\n\n### Recommended changes\n\n1. **Pin main for calibration regardless of `--pin`.** Even when the\n   user didn't specify `--pin`, briefly pin main (to `pin_cores[0]`\n   if set, otherwise a fixed default such as core 0, or a new\n   `--cal-pin` flag) so calibration always runs in a stable\n   environment. Biggest coherence win — calibration stops depending\n   on how the bench is configured.\n2. **Longer warmup.** Bump `CAL_WARMUP` from 1,000 to ~100,000\n   iterations so boost ramp completes before `measure(N_LOW)` runs.\n3. **More samples.** Bump `CAL_SAMPLES` from 10,000 to ~100,000 so\n   the min-of-samples estimator reliably hits the theoretical floor.\n4. **Wider N spread.** Raise `N_HIGH` to ~10,000 (or drop `N_LOW` to\n   ~10). This drops the noise-amplification coefficient on `min_low`\n   from 1.11× to ~1.01×, so any remaining endpoint noise barely\n   perturbs framing.\n5. *(Optional)* **Sanity-check retry.** Run calibration 3–5 times;\n   require framing/loop to agree within a tolerance. Retry or warn\n   otherwise. Belt-and-suspenders for the rare bad run.\n\n### Dev steps (TBD — awaiting approval on single-step vs multi-step)\n\nMulti-step probably fits better because (1)–(4) are independent and\nwe'll want a before/after measurement at each step. Rough order:\n\n- `0.6.0-dev1` — chore marker: bump version, write this plan, update\n  todo.\n- `0.6.0-dev2` — pin main for calibration regardless of `--pin`.\n- `0.6.0-dev3` — longer warmup + more samples.\n- `0.6.0-dev4` — widen N spread.\n- `0.6.0-dev5` *(optional)* — sanity-check retry loop.\n- `0.6.0` final — remove `-devN`, update todo/chores.\n"
}

Yes — saw "I like the recommendation" as approval, chores-02 was written on that basis. The todo.md edits were the ones you interrupted to add `--no-pin-cal`.

Both additions make sense and will go in:
- **`--no-pin-cal`** — keeps the current (unpinned when user didn't pass `--pin`) behavior available for A/B comparison after the default flips. Exactly the right way to land the change: make the new behavior default, keep an opt-out to regression-test against.
- **min-p1 & p1-p10 are real.** Yes — when both round-trip endpoints stay hot and never park (scheduler co-locates them on the same CCX, unparked, spinning on the channel), `std::sync::mpsc` (crossbeam-channel under the hood) can hit sub-µs round-trips. Pinning to `5,10` on the 3900X likely forces cross-CCX, which raises the floor. Worth calling out in the chore so future readers don't mistake those bands for calibration artifacts.

Incorporating now.

That\ngap isn't calibration noise or a measurement artifact.\n\nWhen both round-trip endpoints stay hot (scheduler co-locates them\non the same CCX, neither parks in a futex, both spin on the\nchannel), `std::sync::mpsc` — `crossbeam-channel` under the hood on\nmodern Rust — can round-trip in well under a microsecond. Pinning\nto `5,10` on a 3900X likely forces cross-CCX placement (cores 5 and\n10 are on different chiplets), which adds cache-coherence latency\nand raises the floor. So the low bands in unpinned runs are a real\noperating regime — \"both ends hot and spinning\" — not a glitch, and\nthe calibration rework here should not distort them."
}

chore: plan 0.6.0 calibration robustness (0.6.0-dev1)

on mpsc-2t --pin 5,10, reverse-engineered it as two-point-fit
amplification of ~10 ns of min_low noise (10/9 coefficient), ran
mpsc-2t pinned vs unpinned to quantify the --pin effect on
calibration stability, and drafted the 0.6.0 plan — including
the --no-pin-cal opt-out for A/B comparison and the note that the
low min-p1/p1-p10 bands are real crossbeam-under-spinning behavior,
not noise.

ochid: /pwrppkpnkqsr
```

**Session repo title & body:**
```
chore: plan 0.6.0 calibration robustness (0.6.0-dev1)

Session where we diagnosed and planned the calibration rework.

- Spotted mpsc-2t --pin 5,10 framing flipping between 11.11 and
  22.22 ns run-to-run
- Reverse-engineered the "exact 2×" as two-point-fit amplification
  of ~10 ns of min_low noise (10/9 coefficient) — not physical
- Ran mpsc-2t pinned vs unpinned to quantify the --pin effect on
  calibration stability (framing 11.11 vs 27.78 ns; stdev 787 vs
  12,967 ns; p99.99 tail 272 µs vs 5.5 ms)
- Drafted the 0.6.0 plan in chores-02: pin main for calibration
  + --no-pin-cal opt-out, longer warmup, more samples, wider N
- Noted the low min-p1/p1-p10 bands are real crossbeam-when-
  spinning behavior, not calibration artifacts
- Recorded commit-message style preference (bullets over
  paragraphs) in CLAUDE.md

ochid: /pwrppkpnkqsr
```

  "old_string": "    /// Pin bench threads to logical CPUs (comma-separated, ranges OK).\n    /// The list is a core *pool*: thread `i` of a bench is pinned to\n    /// `pool[i % pool.len()]`, so shorter pools oversubscribe by wrap.\n    /// Examples: `--pin 0,1` (2 threads → 2 CPUs), `--pin 0-5` (6-thread\n    /// pool), `--pin 0,0` (two threads on the same CPU). On 3900X,\n    /// logical CPUs N and N+12 are SMT siblings of the same physical\n    /// core — `--pin 0,12` pairs siblings (max contention), `--pin 0,1`\n    /// gives independent cores. Omit to leave threads unpinned.\n    #[arg(long, value_name = \"CORES\")]\n    pin: Option<String>,\n}",
  "new_string": "    /// Pin bench threads to logical CPUs (comma-separated, ranges OK).\n    /// The list is a core *pool*: thread `i` of a bench is pinned to\n    /// `pool[i % pool.len()]`, so shorter pools oversubscribe by wrap.\n    /// Examples: `--pin 0,1` (2 threads → 2 CPUs), `--pin 0-5` (6-thread\n    /// pool), `--pin 0,0` (two threads on the same CPU). On 3900X,\n    /// logical CPUs N and N+12 are SMT siblings of the same physical\n    /// core — `--pin 0,12` pairs siblings (max contention), `--pin 0,1`\n    /// gives independent cores. Omit to leave threads unpinned.\n    #[arg(long, value_name = \"CORES\")]\n    pin: Option<String>,\n\n    /// Skip pinning the main thread for calibration. By default,\n    /// calibration always runs pinned (to `pin[0]` if set, else\n    /// core 0) so framing/loop numbers come from a stable\n    /// environment regardless of `--pin`. Pass this flag to\n    /// reproduce the pre-0.6.0 behavior (main pinned only when\n    /// `--pin` is given) — useful for A/B comparing calibration\n    /// stability. No effect when `--pin` is set (main stays pinned\n    /// to `pin[0]` either way).\n    #[arg(long)]\n    no_pin_cal: bool,\n}"
}

  "old_string": "    // Pin the main (measurement) thread before any calibration or bench\n    // work so TSC reads stay on the same CPU across start/elapsed.\n    if let Some(&cpu) = pin_cores.first() {\n        pin::pin_current(Some(cpu));\n    }\n\n    let overhead = overhead::calibrate();\n    println!(\"Calibration:\");\n    println!(\n        \"  framing/sample    {:>7} ns  (timer pair, two-point fit)\",\n        harness::fmt_commas_f64(overhead.framing_per_sample_ns, 2)\n    );\n    println!(\n        \"  loop/iter         {:>7} ns  (per inner-loop iteration)\",\n        harness::fmt_commas_f64(overhead.loop_per_iter_ns, 2)\n    );\n    println!(\"  pinning           {}\", pin::plan_summary(&pin_cores));\n    println!();",
  "new_string": "    // Pin main for calibration. Always pinned by default so\n    // framing/loop numbers come from a stable environment\n    // regardless of --pin. --no-pin-cal restores pre-0.6.0\n    // behavior (pin iff --pin given).\n    let cal_pin: Option<usize> = pin_cores\n        .first()\n        .copied()\n        .or(if cli.no_pin_cal { None } else { Some(0) });\n    pin::pin_current(cal_pin);\n\n    let overhead = overhead::calibrate();\n    let cal_pin_display = match (pin_cores.first(), cli.no_pin_cal) {\n        (Some(c), _) => format!(\"core {c} (from --pin)\"),\n        (None, true) => \"none (--no-pin-cal)\".to_string(),\n        (None, false) => \"core 0 (default; --no-pin-cal to disable)\".to_string(),\n    };\n    println!(\"Calibration:\");\n    println!(\n        \"  framing/sample    {:>7} ns  (timer pair, two-point fit)\",\n        harness::fmt_commas_f64(overhead.framing_per_sample_ns, 2)\n    );\n    println!(\n        \"  loop/iter         {:>7} ns  (per inner-loop iteration)\",\n        harness::fmt_commas_f64(overhead.loop_per_iter_ns, 2)\n    );\n    println!(\"  cal pin           {cal_pin_display}\");\n    println!(\"  bench pin         {}\", pin::plan_summary(&pin_cores));\n    println!();"
}

id: toolu_0156DdVQCWRYqYG65RSUz3VB
```
54	- `--pin CORES` — pin bench threads to logical CPUs. `CORES` is a
55	  comma-separated list with optional ranges: `0,1`, `0-5`, `0,3-5,7`.
56	  Treated as a **core pool** indexed positionally with wrap-around, so
57	  thread `i` gets `pool[i % pool.len()]`. Examples:
58	  - `--pin 0,1` pins a 2-thread bench to logical CPUs 0 and 1.
59	  - `--pin 0,0` co-locates two threads on the same CPU
60	    (oversubscription — measures contention).
61	  - `--pin 0-11` defines a 12-CPU pool for larger fanout benches;
62	    threads wrap over it.
63	
64	  On AMD Zen 2 (e.g. Ryzen 9 3900X, 12 physical × 2 SMT = 24 logical
65	  CPUs), logical IDs `N` and `N+12` are SMT siblings of the same
66	  physical core. `--pin 0,12` pairs siblings (max resource contention);
67	  `--pin 0,1` uses independent physical cores in the same CCX (best
68	  latency for channel benches — shared L3, no SMT contention). Check
69	  your topology with
70	  `cat /sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu0/topology/thread_siblings_list`.
71	
72	  Typical measured effect on `mpsc-2t` at `-d 10` (3900X, idle desktop):
73	  unpinned mean ≈ 7,044 ns / stdev ≈ 6,545 ns / p99.99 ≈ 74 µs;
74	  `--pin 0,1` → mean ≈ 5,636 ns / stdev ≈ 1,321 ns / p99.99 ≈ 17 µs.
75	  Tail tightens ~4×, stdev ~5×, mean drops ~20 %.
76	
77	Each bench prints a band-based histogram in nanoseconds. Bands are
78	defined by percentile boundaries (min→p1, p1→p10, …, p99→max) and
79	show first, last, range (`last - first + 1`), count, mean, and
80	adjusted mean. The adjusted column subtracts apparatus overhead
81	(`framing_per_sample / inner + loop_per_iter`), calibrated once at
82	startup via a two-point fit on an empty bench.
83	
84	Examples:
85	
86	```
87	iiac-perf                        # list available benches
88	iiac-perf all                    # every bench, default ~5s each
```

> AGENT

`0.6.0-dev2` implementation complete:

**Code changes:**
- `Cargo.toml`: `0.6.0-dev1` → `0.6.0-dev2`
- `src/main.rs`:
  - New `--no-pin-cal` CLI flag
  - `cal_pin` derivation: `pin[0]` if `--pin` set, else `Some(0)` by default or `None` with `--no-pin-cal`
  - Startup banner: new `cal pin` line; renamed `pinning` → `bench pin` for clarity

**Docs:**
- `README.md`: `--no-pin-cal` entry in flag list; note about `cal pin` / `bench pin` banner lines
- `notes/chores-02.md`: dev1 ✅, dev2 ✅, plus note that dev2 fixes *coherence* only (stability comes in dev3/dev4)

**A/B verification across four combos** (`mpsc-2t -d 2`):
| run | command | cal pin line |
|-----|---------|--------------|
| A | `--pin 5,10` | `core 5 (from --pin)` |
| B | `--pin 5,10 --no-pin-cal` | `core 5 (from --pin)` *(--pin wins as intended)* |
| C | *(no flags)* | `core 0 (default; --no-pin-cal to disable)` |
| D | `--no-pin-cal` | `none (--no-pin-cal)` |

Framing values still wobble (11.11 / 14.44 / 22.22 / 11.11 in this 4-sample set) because dev2 doesn't touch the warmup, sample count, or N-spread knobs — those come next. Coherence is the win here.

`cargo fmt` clean, `clippy` clean, 8 tests pass, installed, all four combos verified in the shell. Ready for review.

Makes A/B comparison easy — run the same\n   bench with and without `--no-pin-cal` to see the calibration\n   difference directly, and gives a quick escape hatch if the new\n   default ever misbehaves on someone else's box.\n\n   **Open question — semantics of `--pin` + `--no-pin-cal`.** As\n   shipped in dev2, `--pin` wins: with `--pin 5,10 --no-pin-cal`\n   main is still pinned to core 5 for calibration. Alternative\n   reading: `--no-pin-cal` should *always* skip the cal pin, so\n   `--pin 5,10 --no-pin-cal` → bench threads pinned to 5,10,\n   calibration runs unpinned. feat: pin main for calibration + --no-pin-cal (0.6.0-dev2)

environment regardless of --pin. New --no-pin-cal flag restores
pre-0.6.0 behavior for A/B comparison. Fixes coherence only;
stability work is dev3/dev4.

  note that --pin + --no-pin-cal may later mean "pin bench but not
  cal" (currently --pin wins)

cal pin banner line and the expected pinning behavior.

> AGENT

Same 10/9 amplification — not a new effect, just a cleaner view of it now that dev3 let the best case drop to the true floor.

Reverse-engineered:
- Run 1: `min_low=50, min_high=490` → framing = (500−490)/9 = **1.11**
- Run 2: `min_low=60, min_high=500` → framing = (600−500)/9 = **11.11**

Note both `min_low` *and* `min_high` shifted by +10 ns from run 1 to run 2 — the entire measurement regime slid up by 10 ns. And since `d(framing)/d(min_low) = 10/9` and `d(framing)/d(min_high) = −1/9`, a simultaneous +10 on both produces `10/9·10 − 1/9·10 = +10` on framing. That's why it's exactly 1.11 → 11.11.

So what we're seeing:
- **Before dev3**: baseline was ~11 ns (under-warmed CPU) and noise bumped it to ~22 — the "exact 2×" illusion
- **After dev3**: baseline is ~1–5 ns (true hardware floor; warmup is now enough) but the same ~10 ns of noise still happens, just around a lower mean. Now it looks like "~1" vs "~11" which is a more dramatic-looking ratio even though the absolute noise is identical

Likely source of the +10 ns shift: core 0 receiving a bit of background IRQ / kernel-timer work during run 2 that touched every sample floor. Core 0 is the default IRQ target on Linux — on desktop kernels it sees the most housekeeping. The fact that the bench itself (mean 6,343 vs 6,181 ns, stdev 1,506 vs 1,232) is stable between these two runs confirms it's localized to the calibration measurement, not a real workload effect.

The good news: dev4 attacks this directly. Widening `N_HIGH` from 1,000 to ~10,000 drops the amplification coefficient on `min_low` from 10/9 ≈ 1.11 to 10,000/9,900 ≈ 1.01 — a ~11× reduction. A 10 ns floor shift on `min_low` would then produce only ~1 ns of framing movement instead of ~11.

Want me to proceed to `0.6.0-dev4`, or first commit dev3 and checkpoint?

`0.6.0-dev4` complete:

**Code changes:**
- `Cargo.toml`: `0.6.0-dev3` → `0.6.0-dev4`
- `src/overhead.rs`: `N_HIGH` 1_000 → 10_000; expanded comment with the `d(framing)/d(min_low) = N_HIGH/(N_HIGH−N_LOW)` derivation and the new ~1.01 coefficient

**Docs:**
- `notes/chores-02.md`: dev4 ✅ with before/after data

**Validation** — `mpsc-2t` 10 runs each:
- **Pinned (`--pin 5,10`)**: 11.12, 11.22, 5.47, 5.17, 5.47, 5.47, 5.47, 5.47, 5.47, 5.47 — steady-state spread ≈ **0.3 ns**
- **Default (core 0)**: 11.12, 11.12, 5.98, 5.68, 5.68, 5.68, 5.68, 5.68, 5.78, 5.68 — steady-state spread ≈ **0.3 ns**

Two observations:
1. Steady state is ~30× tighter than dev3 (0.3 ns vs ~10 ns spread) — exactly the ~10× amplification improvement the fit predicted
2. First one or two runs in a batch still show +6 ns — this is CPU coming out of a deep C-state between runs (100k warmup iters ≈ 100 µs is short vs C6 exit latency of 100-300 µs). Could be addressed separately, but doesn't affect back-to-back runs.

Cal time: ~60 ms → ~510 ms one-time at startup (10× longer N_HIGH samples). `cargo fmt` clean, `clippy` clean, 8 tests pass, installed. Ready for review.

wink@3900x 26-04-16T19:53:45.945Z:~/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf ((main))

> AGENT

Nice catch — that's a more nuanced finding than our earlier chores-02 analysis admitted.

**What's happening:** with `--no-pin-cal` (main unpinned entirely), bench threads get scheduler flexibility and land where co-location is best — same CCX, shared L3 — hitting the spin-spin fast path (min-p1 at **130–410 ns**, sub-µs). With the default (main pinned to core 0), the scheduler treats core 0 as taken even though main is parked in `join`, and pushes bench threads elsewhere — raising the floor (min-p1 3,507–3,567 ns) but tightening the tail (stdev 1,258 vs 3,047, p99.99 257 µs vs 1.02 ms).

**Implication for chores-02:** my earlier "main's pin state is a don't-care because it blocks on join" was wrong for the *unpinned-bench* case. The scheduler does use main's affinity as a placement hint for unrelated threads. For *pinned* benches it really is a don't-care (bench threads are already fixed).

**Small refinement that would give us best-of-both:** pin main *only during calibration*, then unpin before the bench starts — but only when `--pin` is absent. That preserves dev2–dev4's stable calibration *and* lets the scheduler work its magic during the bench (fast-path visible in defaults, not just with `--no-pin-cal`).

Logic sketch:
- `--pin X,Y` given → pin main to X through calibration *and* bench (unchanged; don't-care anyway)
- No `--pin` (default) → pin main to core 0 for calibration, then `pin::pin_current` to reset affinity before bench spawn
- `--no-pin-cal` → no cal pin, as today

Thoughts? If yes, I'd fold it in as an amendment to dev2 semantics — could be a small `0.6.0-dev5` or part of the existing dev5 retry-loop step. Or roll it into the `0.6.0` final if you want to keep dev5 for the retry.

Want me to commit dev4 first, then we discuss dev5 / this refinement?

1	use std::collections::BTreeSet;
2	
3	/// Parse a `--pin` value (comma-separated list with optional ranges) into
4	/// an ordered vector of logical CPU IDs. Accepts forms like `"0,1"`,
5	/// `"0-11"`, `"0,3-5,7"`. Duplicates are preserved in the given order so
6	/// oversubscription (`"0,0,0"`) works.
7	pub fn parse_cores(spec: &str) -> Result<Vec<usize>, String> {
8	    let mut out = Vec::new();
9	    for part in spec.split(',').map(str::trim).filter(|s| !s.is_empty()) {
10	        match part.split_once('-') {
11	            None => out.push(
12	                part.parse::<usize>()
13	                    .map_err(|e| format!("invalid core id {part:?}: {e}"))?,
14	            ),
15	            Some((lo, hi)) => {
16	                let lo = lo
17	                    .trim()
18	                    .parse::<usize>()
19	                    .map_err(|e| format!("invalid range start {lo:?}: {e}"))?;
20	                let hi = hi
21	                    .trim()
22	                    .parse::<usize>()
23	                    .map_err(|e| format!("invalid range end {hi:?}: {e}"))?;
24	                if hi < lo {
25	                    return Err(format!("range {lo}-{hi} is empty"));
26	                }
27	                out.extend(lo..=hi);
28	            }
29	        }
30	    }
31	    Ok(out)
32	}
33	
34	/// Print the current thread's CPU id to stderr with a label.
35	/// Useful for debugging pinning — not called in normal paths.
36	#[allow(dead_code)]
37	pub fn print_core_id(prompt: &str) {
38	    let cid = unsafe { libc::sched_getcpu() };
39	    eprintln!("{prompt}: cid={cid}");
40	}
41	
42	/// Pin the current thread to `logical_cpu`. No-op if `None`.
43	///
44	/// Constructs a `CoreId` directly rather than querying
45	/// `get_core_ids()`, which only returns cores in the caller's
46	/// current affinity mask — after the first pin that mask is
47	/// narrowed and subsequent lookups for other cores would fail.
48	pub fn pin_current(logical_cpu: Option<usize>) {
49	    let Some(target) = logical_cpu else { return };
50	    core_affinity::set_for_current(core_affinity::CoreId { id: target });
51	}
52	
53	/// Report the pinning plan (what the user asked for) as a human-readable
54	/// summary for the startup banner.
55	pub fn plan_summary(cores: &[usize]) -> String {
56	    if cores.is_empty() {
57	        return "none (unpinned)".to_string();
58	    }
59	    let unique: BTreeSet<usize> = cores.iter().copied().collect();
60	    format!(
61	        "{cores:?} ({} slot{}, {} unique CPU{})",
62	        cores.len(),
63	        if cores.len() == 1 { "" } else { "s" },
64	        unique.len(),
65	        if unique.len() == 1 { "" } else { "s" },
66	    )
67	}
68

  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "/// Pin the current thread to `logical_cpu`. No-op if `None`.\n///\n/// Constructs a `CoreId` directly rather than querying\n/// `get_core_ids()`, which only returns cores in the caller's\n/// current affinity mask — after the first pin that mask is\n/// narrowed and subsequent lookups for other cores would fail.\npub fn pin_current(logical_cpu: Option<usize>) {\n    let Some(target) = logical_cpu else { return };\n    core_affinity::set_for_current(core_affinity::CoreId { id: target });\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Pin the current thread to `logical_cpu`. No-op if `None`.\n///\n/// Constructs a `CoreId` directly rather than querying\n/// `get_core_ids()`, which only returns cores in the caller's\n/// current affinity mask — after the first pin that mask is\n/// narrowed and subsequent lookups for other cores would fail.\npub fn pin_current(logical_cpu: Option<usize>) {\n    let Some(target) = logical_cpu else { return };\n    core_affinity::set_for_current(core_affinity::CoreId { id: target });\n}\n\n/// Snapshot the current thread's CPU affinity mask. Returned opaque\n/// value can be fed to [`restore_affinity`] to undo a later pin.\n/// Returns `None` if the syscall fails (very unusual on Linux).\npub fn save_affinity() -> Option<libc::cpu_set_t> {\n    let mut set: libc::cpu_set_t = unsafe { std::mem::zeroed() };\n    let ret = unsafe { libc::sched_getaffinity(0, size_of::<libc::cpu_set_t>(), &mut set) };\n    (ret == 0).then_some(set)\n}\n\n/// Restore a previously-saved affinity mask. Used after calibration\n/// to widen the mask back to what we started with (typically \"all\n/// CPUs the process was launched with\", preserving any outer\n/// `taskset` restrictions).\npub fn restore_affinity(set: &libc::cpu_set_t) {\n    unsafe {\n        libc::sched_setaffinity(0, size_of::<libc::cpu_set_t>(), set);\n    }\n}"
}

Always pinned by default so\n    // framing/loop numbers come from a stable environment\n    // regardless of --pin. --no-pin-cal restores pre-0.6.0\n    // behavior (pin iff --pin given).\n    let cal_pin: Option<usize> =\n        pin_cores\n            .first()\n            .copied()\n            .or(if cli.no_pin_cal { None } else { Some(0) });\n    pin::pin_current(cal_pin);\n\n    let overhead = overhead::calibrate();\n    let cal_pin_display = match (pin_cores.first(), cli.no_pin_cal) {\n        (Some(c), _) => format!(\"core {c} (from --pin)\"),\n        (None, true) => \"none (--no-pin-cal)\".to_string(),\n        (None, false) => \"core 0 (default; --no-pin-cal to disable)\".to_string(),\n    };",

Always pinned by default so\n    // framing/loop numbers come from a stable environment\n    // regardless of --pin. --no-pin-cal restores pre-0.6.0\n    // behavior (pin iff --pin given).\n    //\n    // When --pin is absent (cal-only pin), we snapshot the\n    // pre-calibration affinity mask and restore it after calibration\n    // so the scheduler can freely place bench threads — otherwise\n    // main's pin leaks into the scheduler's co-location decisions and\n    // suppresses the \"both ends hot and spinning\" fast path.\n    let cal_pin: Option<usize> =\n        pin_cores\n            .first()\n            .copied()\n            .or(if cli.no_pin_cal { None } else { Some(0) });\n    let saved_affinity = if pin_cores.is_empty() && !cli.no_pin_cal {\n        pin::save_affinity()\n    } else {\n        None\n    };\n    pin::pin_current(cal_pin);\n\n    let overhead = overhead::calibrate();\n\n    if let Some(set) = saved_affinity.as_ref() {\n        pin::restore_affinity(set);\n    }\n\n    let cal_pin_display = match (pin_cores.first(), cli.no_pin_cal) {\n        (Some(c), _) => format!(\"core {c} (from --pin)\"),\n        (None, true) => \"none (--no-pin-cal)\".to_string(),\n        (None, false) => \"core 0 (unpinned after cal; --no-pin-cal to skip)\".to_string(),\n    };"
}

> DEVELOPER

This is good, I said no because I'd like to add a -v,--verbose as I'd like to see the save/restore_affinity and also some output on the calibration phase this will allow me and future people to get a better insight on what's happening, your thoughts, no code

> AGENT

Good idea — makes implicit scheduler decisions legible, which is half the battle with benchmarks.

**What to put behind `-v`:**
- **Affinity lifecycle** — saved mask (pre-cal), pinned mask, restored mask, current CPU (`sched_getcpu`). Compressed range format like `0-23` (24 cpus) when full, `{0}` when singleton.
- **Calibration parameters** — `CAL_WARMUP`, `CAL_SAMPLES`, `N_LOW`, `N_HIGH`, noise-amp coefficient (`N_HIGH/(N_HIGH-N_LOW)`).
- **Calibration raw** — `min_low`, `min_high` (integer ns, straight from `measure()`).
- **Calibration derived** — full-precision `framing` and `loop_per_iter` (not rounded), and the calibration wall-clock duration.

**Design choices worth your call:**
1. **Placement:** a new `Verbose (calibration):` block printed right after `Calibration:` (keeps normal output clean, so parsers don't break). Goes to stdout alongside the rest of the banner.
2. **Where diagnostics live:** extend the `Overhead` struct with the raw/timing fields (always populated), then `main` prints them iff `-v`. Keeps `overhead.rs` free of formatting and avoids threading a verbose flag through calibration.
3. **Scope cap for this change:** cal + affinity only. Bench-thread runtime placement (which core each thread actually ran on; migration events) is a separate feature — more files touched, and sampling `sched_getcpu` during a bench can perturb the measurement. Defer unless you want it now.

**Also:** should `-v` be `--verbose` (bool) or a level like `-v / -vv` (count)? Bool is simplest and probably enough — we can always introduce levels later if a future feature really needs finer-grained output.

Sound good as-is, or want to tweak scope/placement? Then I'll implement, along with the unpin-after-cal change, and commit it all as part of dev4.

> DEVELOPER

single level for now is fine so -v,--verbose, I'd also like info!, debug! .. capability and one other thing I'll probably be moving this to musl runtime and static linking if that can work, thoughts

> AGENT

**`-v` + `log` macros:** natural fit, standard Rust pattern.

- Add `log = "0.4"` and `env_logger = "0.11"` as deps.
- Wire `-v` to set `env_logger` filter: no flag → `warn` (near-silent), `-v` → `debug` (shows `info!` and `debug!`).
- Also honors `RUST_LOG` env var for free, so users can do `RUST_LOG=debug iiac-perf ...` or filter per-module.
- Mental model: use `info!` for the lifecycle things a user might want to see (affinity save/restore, cal params), `debug!` for deeper details (raw min_low/min_high ticks, precise fit numbers). Both show under `-v`.
- Default `warn` means the binary is silent in scripts unless something's actually wrong — good for piping/parsing.

**One small tension:** the existing `Calibration:` banner block prints to *stdout* via `println!`. If we move cal diagnostics into `info!`/`debug!`, those go to *stderr* by default. That's actually fine — stdout stays parseable, verbose output is clearly separable. But it means the `Calibration:` summary and the `-v` details land on different streams. Livable, I think.

**musl static linking:** should work without code changes. Every current dep is either pure Rust or libc-only:
- `libc`, `clap`, `core_affinity`, `hdrhistogram`, `minstant` — all compile against musl
- `sched_setaffinity`/`sched_getaffinity` and `CPU_SET` macros exist in musl's libc
- `rdtsc` in `minstant` is inline asm, unaffected by libc
- `log` + `env_logger` are pure Rust

Build story: `rustup target add x86_64-unknown-linux-musl`, then `cargo build --release --target x86_64-unknown-linux-musl`. Binary ends up bigger (~1–2 MB vs ~500 KB glibc-dynamic) but fully portable — copy it to any Linux box including containers. For an IIAC perf tool specifically, musl's allocator performance doesn't matter here: the hot path is a spin-spinny channel round-trip, not malloc-heavy.

**Worth considering now vs later:**
- **Do in this change:** `-v` + log deps + `info!`/`debug!` usage for cal + affinity. Natural scope with the verbose work.
- **Defer separately:** musl target. It's a CI / release-engineering concern, not dev4 territory. Adding a `.cargo/config.toml` target default or a musl-tagged release could be its own chore later. No code impact for this change.

Scope for dev4 as-is then: unpin-after-cal + save/restore helpers + `-v, --verbose` + `log` + `env_logger` + `info!`/`debug!` plumbing. Sound right, or widen/narrow?

My take: split into two clean steps, with the logging infra going *first* so the behavioral change can use it for observability.

**Recommended ordering:**
- `0.6.0-dev4` ✅ (as already done) — widen N spread. Commit now.
- `0.6.0-dev5` — `-v/--verbose` + `log` + `env_logger` + `info!`/`debug!` plumbing throughout (cal params, raw min_low/min_high, etc.). No behavior change — pure observability.
- `0.6.0-dev6` — unpin-after-cal + `pin::save_affinity`/`restore_affinity` helpers. Now the `info!` calls show the mask before/after the restore so you can *see* the unpin happening in `-v` output. Before/after data on the fast-path restoration lands with the commit.
- `0.6.0-dev7` *(was dev5, optional)* — sanity-check retry loop.
- `0.6.0` final — remove `-devN`, update notes.

**Why this order:** doing log infra first lets the unpin-after-cal change be *self-documenting* in the commit message and in user-visible `-v` output ("saved affinity {0-23}, pinned {0}, restored {0-23}"). If we do the behavioral change first, we'd later need a second pass to thread `info!` through the same paths.

musl stays as a separate, later concern — not part of the 0.6.0 chore.

Want me to commit dev4 first (as-is), then we spin up dev5 for logging?

<tool_use_error>String to replace not found in file.
String: /// Pin the current thread to `logical_cpu`. No-op if `None`.
///
/// Constructs a `CoreId` directly rather than querying
/// `get_core_ids()`, which only returns cores in the caller's
/// current affinity mask — after the first pin that mask is
/// narrowed and subsequent lookups for other cores would fail.
pub fn pin_current(logical_cpu: Option<usize>) {
    let Some(target) = logical_cpu else { return };
    core_affinity::set_for_current(core_affinity::CoreId { id: target });
}

/// Snapshot the current thread's CPU affinity mask. Returned opaque
/// value can be fed to [`restore_affinity`] to undo a later pin.
/// Returns `None` if the syscall fails (very unusual on Linux).
pub fn save_affinity() -> Option<libc::cpu_set_t> {
    let mut set: libc::cpu_set_t = unsafe { std::mem::zeroed() };
    let ret = unsafe { libc::sched_getaffinity(0, size_of::<libc::cpu_set_t>(), &mut set) };
    (ret == 0).then_some(set)
}

/// Restore a previously-saved affinity mask. Used after calibration
/// to widen the mask back to what we started with (typically "all
/// CPUs the process was launched with", preserving any outer
/// `taskset` restrictions).
pub fn restore_affinity(set: &libc::cpu_set_t) {
    unsafe {
        libc::sched_setaffinity(0, size_of::<libc::cpu_set_t>(), set);
    }
}</tool_use_error>

Checkpoint-1 commit block for `0.6.0-dev4`:

**App repo title & body:**
```
perf: widen N spread for robust calibration (0.6.0-dev4)

Widening N_HIGH drops the noise-amplification coefficient on
min_low from 10/9 ≈ 1.11 to 10_000/9_900 ≈ 1.01 — a ~10×
reduction. Combined with the longer warmup + more samples in
dev3, steady-state framing is now tight (~0.3 ns spread across
consecutive runs, vs ~10 ns before). First-run-after-idle still
shows a ~6 ns lift (CPU exiting a deep C-state; warmup budget
short relative to C6 exit latency) — separate concern.

- Cargo.toml: 0.6.0-dev3 → 0.6.0-dev4
- src/overhead.rs: N_HIGH 1_000 → 10_000; expanded comment with
  the d(framing)/d(min_low) = N_HIGH/(N_HIGH-N_LOW) derivation
- notes/chores-02.md: mark dev4 ✅ with before/after data

Verified with 10 consecutive runs of `mpsc-2t --pin 5,10 -d 0.5`:
framing settles at 5.47 ns ± 0.3 ns (dev3 was 4.44–14.44).
Calibration time 60 ms → 510 ms (one-time startup cost; the 10×
longer N_HIGH samples).

ochid: /.claude/plovltwkmtws
```

```
perf: widen N spread for robust calibration (0.6.0-dev4)

Session implementing dev4 of the 0.6.0 plan.

- Bumped N_HIGH from 1_000 to 10_000; N_LOW held at 100
- Ran 10× back-to-back to see the tightening; steady-state spread
  dropped from ~10 ns (dev3) to ~0.3 ns
- Observed an unexpected interaction: with default (main pinned to
  core 0 for cal), bench threads lose the sub-µs spin-spin fast
  path (min-p1 ≈ 3.5 µs), while --no-pin-cal preserves it
  (min-p1 ≈ 130 ns) — scheduler uses main's affinity as a
  placement hint even though main is parked. To be addressed in
  a follow-up step (unpin main after cal)
- Planned dev5 (verbose/log infra) and dev6 (unpin-after-cal
  using that infra for visibility) based on the observation
- Discussed musl static-linking viability for a later chore
  (separate from 0.6.0) — all current deps are musl-compatible

ochid: /kylpmmznwxuz
```

30	    /// CPU id for the bench's `thread_idx`-th thread, using wrap-around
31	    /// over the pool. Returns `None` when the pool is empty so callers
32	    /// can treat unpinned and pinned runs uniformly.
33	    pub fn core_for(&self, thread_idx: usize) -> Option<usize> {

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/overhead.rs",
  "old_string": "pub fn calibrate() -> Overhead {\n    let mut bench = EmptyBench;\n    for _ in 0..CAL_WARMUP {\n        black_box(bench.step());\n    }\n\n    let min_low = measure(&mut bench, CAL_SAMPLES, N_LOW);\n    let min_high = measure(&mut bench, CAL_SAMPLES, N_HIGH);\n\n    // Two-point fit: per_sample = framing + inner * loop_per_iter.\n    // Slope between (N_LOW, min_low) and (N_HIGH, min_high) gives loop_per_iter,\n    // intercept gives framing. Cancels TSC pipelining of the framing pair.\n    //\n    // Noise amplification: d(framing)/d(min_low) = N_HIGH / (N_HIGH - N_LOW).\n    // At 100 / 10_000 that's ~1.01, so ~10 ns of slop on min_low produces\n    // only ~10 ns on framing. Previously 100 / 1_000 gave ~1.11, which was\n    // the cause of the \"exact 2×\" framing anomaly pre-0.6.0.\n    let loop_per_iter_ns = if min_high > min_low {\n        (min_high - min_low) as f64 / (N_HIGH - N_LOW) as f64\n    } else {\n        0.0\n    };\n    let framing_per_sample_ns = (min_low as f64 - N_LOW as f64 * loop_per_iter_ns).max(0.0);\n\n    Overhead {\n        framing_per_sample_ns,\n        loop_per_iter_ns,\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "pub fn calibrate() -> Overhead {\n    let mut bench = EmptyBench;\n    let cal_start = Instant::now();\n    for _ in 0..CAL_WARMUP {\n        black_box(bench.step());\n    }\n\n    let min_low = measure(&mut bench, CAL_SAMPLES, N_LOW);\n    let min_high = measure(&mut bench, CAL_SAMPLES, N_HIGH);\n\n    // Two-point fit: per_sample = framing + inner * loop_per_iter.\n    // Slope between (N_LOW, min_low) and (N_HIGH, min_high) gives loop_per_iter,\n    // intercept gives framing. Cancels TSC pipelining of the framing pair.\n    //\n    // Noise amplification: d(framing)/d(min_low) = N_HIGH / (N_HIGH - N_LOW).\n    // At 100 / 10_000 that's ~1.01, so ~10 ns of slop on min_low produces\n    // only ~10 ns on framing. Previously 100 / 1_000 gave ~1.11, which was\n    // the cause of the \"exact 2×\" framing anomaly pre-0.6.0.\n    let loop_per_iter_ns = if min_high > min_low {\n        (min_high - min_low) as f64 / (N_HIGH - N_LOW) as f64\n    } else {\n        0.0\n    };\n    let framing_per_sample_ns = (min_low as f64 - N_LOW as f64 * loop_per_iter_ns).max(0.0);\n\n    Overhead {\n        framing_per_sample_ns,\n        loop_per_iter_ns,\n        cal_min_low_ns: min_low,\n        cal_min_high_ns: min_high,\n        cal_duration: cal_start.elapsed(),\n    }\n}"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/pin.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Pin the current thread to `logical_cpu`. No-op if `None`.\n///\n/// Constructs a `CoreId` directly rather than querying\n/// `get_core_ids()`, which only returns cores in the caller's\n/// current affinity mask — after the first pin that mask is\n/// narrowed and subsequent lookups for other cores would fail.\npub fn pin_current(logical_cpu: Option<usize>) {\n    let Some(target) = logical_cpu else { return };\n    core_affinity::set_for_current(core_affinity::CoreId { id: target });\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Pin the current thread to `logical_cpu`. No-op if `None`.\n///\n/// Constructs a `CoreId` directly rather than querying\n/// `get_core_ids()`, which only returns cores in the caller's\n/// current affinity mask — after the first pin that mask is\n/// narrowed and subsequent lookups for other cores would fail.\npub fn pin_current(logical_cpu: Option<usize>) {\n    let Some(target) = logical_cpu else { return };\n    core_affinity::set_for_current(core_affinity::CoreId { id: target });\n}\n\n/// Read the current thread's CPU affinity mask, or `None` if the\n/// syscall fails (very unusual on Linux).\npub fn current_affinity() -> Option<libc::cpu_set_t> {\n    let mut set: libc::cpu_set_t = unsafe { std::mem::zeroed() };\n    let ret = unsafe { libc::sched_getaffinity(0, size_of::<libc::cpu_set_t>(), &mut set) };\n    (ret == 0).then_some(set)\n}\n\n/// Format a CPU set as a compact range list with a count suffix,\n/// e.g. `\"0-11,13-15 (15 cpus)\"` or `\"5 (1 cpu)\"`.\npub fn affinity_summary(set: &libc::cpu_set_t) -> String {\n    let cpus: Vec<usize> = (0..libc::CPU_SETSIZE as usize)\n        .filter(|&i| unsafe { libc::CPU_ISSET(i, set) })\n        .collect();\n    if cpus.is_empty() {\n        return \"<empty>\".to_string();\n    }\n    let mut ranges: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n    let mut start = cpus[0];\n    let mut prev = cpus[0];\n    for &c in &cpus[1..] {\n        if c == prev + 1 {\n            prev = c;\n        } else {\n            ranges.push(if start == prev {\n                start.to_string()\n            } else {\n                format!(\"{start}-{prev}\")\n            });\n            start = c;\n            prev = c;\n        }\n    }\n    ranges.push(if start == prev {\n        start.to_string()\n    } else {\n        format!(\"{start}-{prev}\")\n    });\n    format!(\n        \"{} ({} cpu{})\",\n        ranges.join(\",\"),\n        cpus.len(),\n        if cpus.len() == 1 { \"\" } else { \"s\" }\n    )\n}"
}

  "new_string": "    /// Skip pinning the main thread for calibration. By default,\n    /// calibration always runs pinned (to `pin[0]` if set, else\n    /// core 0) so framing/loop numbers come from a stable\n    /// environment regardless of `--pin`. Pass this flag to\n    /// reproduce the pre-0.6.0 behavior (main pinned only when\n    /// `--pin` is given) — useful for A/B comparing calibration\n    /// stability. No effect when `--pin` is set (main stays pinned\n    /// to `pin[0]` either way).\n    #[arg(long)]\n    no_pin_cal: bool,\n\n    /// Enable verbose internals on stderr: affinity mask, cal\n    /// parameters and raw fit inputs, calibration wall time.\n    /// Equivalent to `RUST_LOG=debug`. Default is `warn` (silent\n    /// unless something's wrong). `RUST_LOG` overrides this flag\n    /// when set, so per-module filtering still works.\n    #[arg(short, long)]\n    verbose: bool,\n}"

looks good but I thought the default was going to be save/restore affinity and we get the "hot-path" data without --no-pin-cal, also add some debug to save/restore_affinity although we don't have that, we lost it in a revert and you didn't resotre :)

> AGENT

Yes — dev6 is exactly where save/restore + hot-path-by-default lands. Confirming the dev6 scope so there's no surprise:

- **Behavior change:** when `--pin` is absent and `--no-pin-cal` is absent, pin main to core 0 for cal, **then restore** pre-cal affinity before benches spawn. Unpinned benches regain the scheduler freedom → sub-µs spin-spin fast path becomes the default.
- **`pin::save_affinity()` / `pin::restore_affinity()`** — restoring what I reverted earlier (you're right, that was sloppy — I dropped them to keep dev4 tight and never re-added).
- **Logging inside those helpers** — `info!` lines from within `save_affinity`/`restore_affinity` themselves so the before/after mask and the restore event show up under `-v` without main.rs having to call `affinity_summary` explicitly at each site. Something like:
  - `info!("save_affinity: mask={}", affinity_summary(&set))`
  - `info!("restore_affinity: mask={}", affinity_summary(set))`
- **Cal pin banner wording update:** `core 0 (default; --no-pin-cal to disable)` → `core 0 (unpinned after cal; --no-pin-cal to skip)` so the actual behavior is clear.

Taking "looks good" as a nit on dev6 scope rather than final approval on Checkpoint-2 (push is shared state, want to be explicit). **Ready to push/finalize dev5?**

```bash
jj git push --bookmark main -R . && \
vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --squash @,@- --push main --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/iiac-perf/src/pin.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Read the current thread's CPU affinity mask, or `None` if the\n/// syscall fails (very unusual on Linux).\npub fn current_affinity() -> Option<libc::cpu_set_t> {\n    let mut set: libc::cpu_set_t = unsafe { std::mem::zeroed() };\n    let ret = unsafe { libc::sched_getaffinity(0, size_of::<libc::cpu_set_t>(), &mut set) };\n    (ret == 0).then_some(set)\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Read the current thread's CPU affinity mask, or `None` if the\n/// syscall fails (very unusual on Linux).\npub fn current_affinity() -> Option<libc::cpu_set_t> {\n    let mut set: libc::cpu_set_t = unsafe { std::mem::zeroed() };\n    let ret = unsafe { libc::sched_getaffinity(0, size_of::<libc::cpu_set_t>(), &mut set) };\n    (ret == 0).then_some(set)\n}\n\n/// Snapshot the current thread's affinity mask so it can be restored\n/// later. Logs the captured mask at `info` level. Returns `None` on\n/// syscall failure (very unusual on Linux).\npub fn save_affinity() -> Option<libc::cpu_set_t> {\n    match current_affinity() {\n        Some(set) => {\n            log::info!(\"save_affinity: mask={}\", affinity_summary(&set));\n            Some(set)\n        }\n        None => {\n            log::warn!(\"save_affinity: sched_getaffinity failed\");\n            None\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Restore a previously-saved affinity mask. Used after calibration\n/// to widen the mask back to what we started with (typically \"all\n/// CPUs the process was launched with\", preserving any outer\n/// `taskset` restrictions). Logs the restored mask at `info` level.\npub fn restore_affinity(set: &libc::cpu_set_t) {\n    let ret = unsafe { libc::sched_setaffinity(0, size_of::<libc::cpu_set_t>(), set) };\n    if ret == 0 {\n        log::info!(\"restore_affinity: mask={}\", affinity_summary(set));\n    } else {\n        log::warn!(\"restore_affinity: sched_setaffinity failed\");\n    }\n}"
}
```

  "new_string": "    // Pin main for calibration. Always pinned by default so\n    // framing/loop numbers come from a stable environment\n    // regardless of --pin. --no-pin-cal restores pre-0.6.0\n    // behavior (pin iff --pin given).\n    //\n    // When we're doing the cal-only pin (--pin absent, --no-pin-cal\n    // absent), snapshot the pre-cal affinity and restore it after\n    // calibrate() so the scheduler regains freedom to co-locate\n    // bench threads — otherwise main's pin leaks into the\n    // scheduler's placement decisions and suppresses the\n    // \"both ends hot and spinning\" fast path.\n    let cal_pin: Option<usize> =\n        pin_cores\n            .first()\n            .copied()\n            .or(if cli.no_pin_cal { None } else { Some(0) });\n    let saved_affinity = if pin_cores.is_empty() && !cli.no_pin_cal {\n        pin::save_affinity()\n    } else {\n        None\n    };\n    pin::pin_current(cal_pin);\n\n    if let Some(cpu) = cal_pin {\n        info!(\"pinned main to core {cpu} for calibration\");\n    } else {\n        info!(\"cal pin skipped\");\n    }\n    if let Some(mask) = pin::current_affinity() {\n        debug!(\"affinity during cal: {}\", pin::affinity_summary(&mask));\n    }"
}

Hot-path is back in the default case — `min-p1` starts at **200 ns** (default) and **90 ns** (verbose test) vs ~3,500 ns before. The save → pin → cal → restore lifecycle is visible with `-v`:

```
[INFO  iiac_perf::pin] save_affinity: mask=0-23 (24 cpus)

[DEBUG iiac_perf] affinity during cal: 0 (1 cpu)
... cal happens ...
[INFO  iiac_perf::pin] restore_affinity: mask=0-23 (24 cpus)
```

And:
- `--pin 5,10`: no save/restore log (main stays pinned throughout, as intended)
- `--no-pin-cal`: "cal pin skipped", affinity stays 0-23 (unchanged pre-0.6.0)
